     1 +Step ID:
     2 +S01
     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: from the manifold structure of X and the Q-acyclicity hypothesis, it
         computes the full compactly supported rational cohomology of X. The orientability observation and the acyclicit
        y input are support for that single computation.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +Since $\Gamma$ contains a 2-torsion element, $M$ is not a point, so $n\ge1$. Because $M$ is a topological manifo
        ld, its universal cover $X$ is inherently a simply connected, and hence orientable, $n$-dimensional topological
        manifold. By Poincar茅 duality for non-compact orientable manifolds, the compactly supported rational cohomology
         of $X$ is isomorphic to its ordinary rational homology:
     9 +
    10 +$$H_{c}^{k}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong H_{n-k}(X;\mathbb{Q})$$
    11 +
    12 +for all integers $k$. Because $X$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-acyclic by hypothesis, its rational homology is concentrated e
        ntirely in degree zero, with $H_{0}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong\mathbb{Q}$. Consequently, the compactly supported rationa
        l cohomology of $X$ is finite-dimensional and concentrated exclusively in the top degree $n$:
    13 +
    14 +$$H_{c}^{k}(X;\mathbb{Q})\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{Q}&if~k=n,\\ 0&if~k\ne n.\end{cases}$$
    15 +
    16 +Target assertion a_i:
    17 +For the connected universal cover X of the compact n-manifold M, one has
    18 +H_c^k(X;Q) = 0 for k != n and H_c^n(X;Q) ~= Q.
    19 +
    20 +Original route:
    21 +Use that X is a simply connected orientable n-manifold, apply noncompact Poincare duality, and insert the Q-acyc
        licity hypothesis.
    22 +
    23 +Explanation:
    24 +The proof first rules out n = 0 because a point has trivial fundamental group, so a manifold with pi_1(M) contai
        ning 2-torsion must have positive dimension. The universal cover X of an n-manifold is again an n-manifold. Sinc
        e X is simply connected, its orientation double cover must be trivial, so X is orientable. At that point the cla
        imed computation is reduced to a standard duality theorem: compactly supported cohomology of an orientable nonco
        mpact n-manifold is dual to ordinary homology with complementary degree. The Q-acyclicity hypothesis says H_j(X;
        Q) vanishes for every j > 0 and H_0(X;Q) ~= Q because X is connected. Substituting j = n-k into the duality isom
        orphism leaves exactly one surviving compactly supported cohomology group, in top degree n, and that group is on
        e-dimensional over Q.
    25 +
    26 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    27 +
    28 +Assertion:
    29 +H_c^k(X;Q) ~= Q for k = n and H_c^k(X;Q) = 0 for k != n.
    30 +
    31 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    32 +- gamma_1: M is a compact manifold without boundary of dimension n and X = \tilde M is its universal cover.
    33 +- gamma_2: pi_1(M) = Gamma contains an element of order 2, so M is not a point and n >= 1.
    34 +- gamma_3: X is Q-acyclic, i.e. H_0(X;Q) ~= Q and H_j(X;Q) = 0 for all j > 0.
    35 +
    36 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    37 +- sigma_1:
    38 +  exact as-used statement: If N is an R-oriented n-manifold, then the duality map D_N : H_c^k(N;R) -> H_{n-k}(N;
        R) is an isomorphism for all k.
    39 +  proof or precise source: Allen Hatcher, Algebraic Topology, Chapter 3, Theorem 3.35, "The duality map D_M : H_
        c^k(M;R) -> H_{n-k}(M;R) is an isomorphism for all k whenever M is an R-oriented n manifold." Online text: https
        ://pi.math.cornell.edu/~hatcher/AT/ATch3.pdf
    40 +  why it applies here: After observing that X is an orientable n-manifold, this theorem applies with N = X and R
         = Q.
    41 +  status: confirmed
    42 +
    43 +Sigma Support:
    44 +- For sigma_1: the cited theorem is exactly the noncompact Poincare duality statement needed here; no strengthen
        ing is used.
    45 +
    46 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    47 +- t_1: The universal cover of an n-manifold is an n-manifold.
    48 +- t_2: A connected simply connected manifold is orientable because its orientation double cover must be trivial.
    49 +- t_3: Substituting the known homology groups of a Q-acyclic connected space into H_c^k(X;Q) ~= H_{n-k}(X;Q).
    50 +
    51 +Deduction:
    52 +From gamma_1, X is a connected n-manifold. By t_2 and simple connectedness of X, X is orientable. Sigma_1 theref
        ore gives H_c^k(X;Q) ~= H_{n-k}(X;Q) for all k. By gamma_3, H_{n-k}(X;Q) is nonzero only when n-k = 0, i.e. k =
        n, and in that case it is Q. Hence H_c^n(X;Q) ~= Q and H_c^k(X;Q) = 0 for k != n.

     1 +Step ID:
     2 +S02
     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: the algebraic compactly supported Lefschetz number of gamma on X is
        nonzero. The freeness of the action and the one-dimensionality of H_c^n(X;Q) are only the ingredients for that c
        onclusion.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +The fundamental group $\Gamma$ acts on $X$ by deck transformations. Because $M$ is a manifold, this properly dis
        continuous action is strictly free. Therefore, the torsion element $\gamma$ acts on $X$ as a free, finite-order
        homeomorphism. As a homeomorphism of $X$, $\gamma$ induces a linear pullback automorphism $\gamma^{*}$ on the co
        mpactly supported cohomology $H_{c}^{*}(X;\mathbb{Q})$. Because $H_{c}^{n}(X;\mathbb{Q})$ is a 1-dimensional $\m
        athbb{Q}$-vector space and $\gamma^{2}=id$, the induced map $\gamma^{*}$ must act as multiplication by $\pm1$. W
        e evaluate the compactly supported Lefschetz number of $\gamma$, defined algebraically as the alternating sum of
         the traces of the induced maps on cohomology. This collapses to a single non-vanishing term at $k=n$, yielding:
     9 +
    10 +$$L_{c}(\gamma,X)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}(-1)^{k}Tr(\gamma^{*}|H_{c}^{k}(X;\mathbb{Q}))=(-1)^{n}(\pm1)\ne0.$$
    11 +
    12 +Target assertion a_i:
    13 +The compactly supported Lefschetz number L_c(gamma,X) is nonzero.
    14 +
    15 +Original route:
    16 +Use the deck action, the freeness of deck transformations, the one-dimensional top compactly supported cohomolog
        y from S01, and the definition of Lefschetz number.
    17 +
    18 +Explanation:
    19 +Every nontrivial deck transformation of a covering acts without fixed points, so the order-2 element gamma acts
        freely on X. The induced pullback gamma^* on H_c^*(X;Q) preserves degree. By S01 all compactly supported cohomol
        ogy groups vanish except H_c^n(X;Q), which is one-dimensional. Because gamma^2 = id, the linear endomorphism gam
        ma^* on that one-dimensional vector space squares to the identity, hence has eigenvalue +1 or -1. The Lefschetz
        number is the alternating sum of traces over all degrees, so every vanished cohomology group contributes zero an
        d only the top-degree term remains. That surviving trace is +/-1, so the whole Lefschetz number is nonzero.
    20 +
    21 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    22 +
    23 +Assertion:
    24 +L_c(gamma,X) = (-1)^n * Tr(gamma^* | H_c^n(X;Q)) with Tr(gamma^* | H_c^n(X;Q)) = +/-1, hence L_c(gamma,X) != 0.
    25 +
    26 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    27 +- gamma_1: gamma in Gamma has order 2.
    28 +- gamma_2: Gamma acts on X by deck transformations, and deck transformations act freely.
    29 +- gamma_3: From S01, H_c^k(X;Q) = 0 for k != n and H_c^n(X;Q) ~= Q.
    30 +
    31 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    32 +- none.
    33 +
    34 +Sigma Support:
    35 +- none.
    36 +
    37 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    38 +- t_1: A homeomorphism induces pullback maps on compactly supported cohomology.
    39 +- t_2: An endomorphism T of a one-dimensional Q-vector space with T^2 = id is multiplication by +1 or -1.
    40 +- t_3: The compactly supported Lefschetz number is the alternating sum of traces on compactly supported cohomolo
        gy.
    41 +
    42 +Deduction:
    43 +By gamma_2, gamma acts on X freely as a homeomorphism of finite order 2. By t_1, it induces gamma^* on H_c^*(X;Q
        ). By gamma_3, the only nonzero compactly supported cohomology group is H_c^n(X;Q), which is one-dimensional ove
        r Q. Since gamma^2 = id by gamma_1, t_2 gives Tr(gamma^* | H_c^n(X;Q)) = +/-1. Applying t_3 and using gamma_3 el
        iminates every other degree, so
    44 +L_c(gamma,X) = (-1)^n Tr(gamma^* | H_c^n(X;Q)) = (-1)^n(+/-1) != 0.

     1 +Step ID:
     2 +S03
     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: for Y = X/<gamma>, compactly supported cohomology identifies with th
        e gamma-invariant subspace upstairs, and therefore chi_c(Y) = (chi_c(X) + L_c(gamma,X))/2. The quotient construc
        tion and linear algebra both serve that one formula.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +On the other hand, we can compute $L_{c}(\gamma,X)$ topologically. Since $\gamma$ generates a strictly free acti
        on of $\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$ on $X$, the quotient $Y=X/\langle\gamma\rangle$ is a non-compact manifold, and th
        e projection $X\rightarrow Y$ is a 2-sheeted regular covering map. The transfer map for compactly supported rati
        onal cohomology yields an isomorphism $H_{c}^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})\cong H_{c}^{*}(X;\mathbb{Q})^{\gamma}$. Because $
        H_{c}^{*}(X;\mathbb{Q})$ is finite-dimensional, $H_{c}^{*}(Y;\mathbb{Q})$ is finite-dimensional, so its compactl
        y supported Euler characteristic $\chi_{c}(Y)$ is a well-defined integer. Algebraically, the dimension of this i
        nvariant subspace dictates $\dim H_{c}^{k}(Y;\mathbb{Q})=\frac{1}{2}(\dim_{\mathbb{Q}} H_{c}^{k}(X;\mathbb{Q})+T
        r(\gamma^{*}|H_{c}^{k}(X;\mathbb{Q})))$. Taking the alternating sum over all degrees yields the relation:
     9 +
    10 +$$\chi_{c}(Y)=\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{c}(X)+L_{c}(\gamma,X)).$$
    11 +
    12 +Target assertion a_i:
    13 +For the free involution gamma on X and Y = X/<gamma>, one has
    14 +chi_c(Y) = (chi_c(X) + L_c(gamma,X)) / 2.
    15 +
    16 +Original route:
    17 +Pass to the 2-sheeted quotient Y, identify compactly supported cohomology downstairs with the invariant part ups
        tairs, then use the dimension formula for an involution.
    18 +
    19 +Explanation:
    20 +Because gamma acts freely, Y is a manifold and X -> Y is a regular double covering. The key point is that compac
        tly supported cochains on Y pull back to gamma-invariant compactly supported cochains on X, and conversely an in
        variant compactly supported cochain upstairs descends uniquely to the quotient because gamma-invariance removes
        the ambiguity in the choice of lift. Hence compactly supported cohomology downstairs is exactly the invariant co
        mpactly supported cohomology upstairs. Over Q, an involution on a finite-dimensional vector space decomposes int
        o +1 and -1 eigenspaces, so the dimension of the +1 eigenspace equals (dim + trace)/2. Applying this in each deg
        ree and summing with the Lefschetz signs gives the announced Euler-characteristic identity.
    21 +
    22 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    23 +
    24 +Assertion:
    25 +H_c^*(Y;Q) ~= H_c^*(X;Q)^gamma and therefore
    26 +chi_c(Y) = (chi_c(X) + L_c(gamma,X))/2.
    27 +
    28 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    29 +- gamma_1: gamma acts freely on X and has order 2.
    30 +- gamma_2: From S01, each H_c^k(X;Q) is finite-dimensional and only finitely many degrees are nonzero.
    31 +- gamma_3: Y denotes X / <gamma>.
    32 +
    33 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    34 +- none.
    35 +
    36 +Sigma Support:
    37 +- none.
    38 +
    39 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    40 +- t_1: A gamma-invariant compactly supported singular cochain on X descends uniquely to a compactly supported si
        ngular cochain on Y = X/<gamma>, and pullback gives the inverse correspondence.
    41 +- t_2: For a finite group acting over a field of characteristic 0, taking invariants commutes with cohomology be
        cause the averaging operator is a chain projection.
    42 +- t_3: For an involution T on a finite-dimensional Q-vector space V, dim(V^{+}) = (dim(V) + Tr(T))/2.
    43 +- t_4: The compactly supported Euler characteristic and Lefschetz number are alternating sums over cohomological
         degrees.
    44 +
    45 +Deduction:
    46 +By gamma_1, X -> Y is a free double cover. For compactly supported cochains, pullback along the quotient map ide
        ntifies C_c^*(Y;Q) with the invariant subcomplex C_c^*(X;Q)^gamma, by t_1. Using t_2 gives
    47 +H_c^*(Y;Q) ~= H(H_c^*(X;Q)^gamma) ~= H_c^*(X;Q)^gamma.
    48 +Finite dimensionality follows from gamma_2. Now apply t_3 degreewise to the involution gamma^* on H_c^k(X;Q):
    49 +dim H_c^k(Y;Q) = dim H_c^k(X;Q)^gamma
    50 +                = (dim H_c^k(X;Q) + Tr(gamma^* | H_c^k(X;Q)))/2.
    51 +Summing these equalities with the alternating sign from t_4 yields
    52 +chi_c(Y) = (chi_c(X) + L_c(gamma,X))/2.

     1 +Step ID:
     2 +S08
     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: the equivariant map f : X -> S is proper.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +**Lemma 1.** The $\Gamma$-equivariant map $f:X\rightarrow S$ is a proper map. *Proof.* Because $\Gamma$ acts coc
        ompactly on both $X$ (as $M$ is compact) and $S$ (as $\Gamma$ is a uniform lattice), we can select compact funda
        mental domains $D_{X}\subset X$ and $D_{S}\subset S$. For any compact subset $K\subset S$, properness of the $\G
        amma$-action on $S$ ensures that the set of group elements $\Phi=\{g\in\Gamma|gf(D_{X})\cap K\ne\emptyset\}$ is
        finite. If $x\in f^{-1}(K)$, then $x=g\cdot y$ for some $g\in\Gamma$ and $y\in D_{X}$. By $\Gamma$-equivariance,
         $f(x)=g\cdot f(y)\in K$, enforcing $g\in\Phi$. Thus, the closed set $f^{-1}(K)$ is contained entirely in the fi
        nite union of compact sets $\bigcup_{g\in\Phi}g\cdot D_{X}$.  Therefore, $f^{-1}(K)$ is definitively compact, es
        tablishing that $f$ is a proper map. $\Box$
     9 +
    10 +Target assertion a_i:
    11 +The Gamma-equivariant map f : X -> S is proper.
    12 +
    13 +Original route:
    14 +Choose compact fundamental domains for the cocompact Gamma-actions and use equivariance to bound f^{-1}(K) insid
        e finitely many translates of a compact set.
    15 +
    16 +Explanation:
    17 +The argument is self-contained. Cocompactness gives a compact set D_X meeting every Gamma-orbit in X. Properness
         of the Gamma-action on S makes only finitely many translates of the compact set f(D_X) meet a fixed compact tar
        get K. Any point x mapping into K can be moved by Gamma into D_X; equivariance then says the corresponding group
         element must belong to that finite set. Hence every point of f^{-1}(K) lies in finitely many translates g D_X.
        Since f^{-1}(K) is closed and those translates are compact, the preimage is compact.
    18 +

     1 +Step ID:
     2 +S19
     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: gamma_S has a fixed point p and, via exp_p, is conjugate to a linear
         involution on T_p S ~= R^d with +1-eigenspace dimension k.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +On the symmetric space $S\cong\mathbb{R}^{d}$, $\gamma_{S}$ acts as an isometric involution. By Cartan's fixed-p
        oint theorem for complete simply connected manifolds of non-positive curvature, the fixed-point set $S^{\gamma_{
        S}}$ is non-empty. Selecting a fixed point $p\in S^{\gamma_{S}}$, the exponential map at $p$ provides a $\gamma_
        {S}$-equivariant diffeomorphism from the tangent space $T_{p}S\cong\mathbb{R}^{d}$ to $S$. Under this identifica
        tion, $\gamma_{S}$ acts as a linear involution. Its $+1$ eigenspace has some dimension $k$ $(0\le k\le d)$ corre
        sponding to the tangent space of $S^{\gamma_{S}}$, and its $-1$ eigenspace has dimension $d-k$.
     9 +
    10 +Target assertion a_i:
    11 +There exists p in S fixed by gamma_S such that, under exp_p : T_p S ~= S, the action of gamma_S is represented b
        y a linear involution of R^d with +1-eigenspace dimension k and -1-eigenspace dimension d-k.
    12 +
    13 +Original route:
    14 +Use Cartan fixed-point theorem, then linearize the isometry at a fixed point through the exponential map.
    15 +
    16 +Explanation:
    17 +This step can be closed directly. Choose any x in S. Because S is Hadamard, there is a unique geodesic segment c
         from x to gamma_S(x). Since gamma_S is an involutive isometry, gamma_S o c is the unique geodesic from gamma_S(
        x) back to x, so gamma_S(c(t)) = c(1-t). Therefore the midpoint p = c(1/2) is fixed by gamma_S. For any isometry
         fixing p one has exp_p o d(gamma_S)_p = gamma_S o exp_p. Since S is Hadamard, the Cartan-Hadamard theorem gives
         that exp_p : T_pS -> S is a diffeomorphism. Hence gamma_S is conjugate to the linear involution d(gamma_S)_p on
         T_pS ~= R^d. Its eigenspace decomposition is then the usual +1/-1 decomposition of an involution.
    18 +
    19 +Statement Extraction `(Gamma_i, Sigma_i, T_i)`:
    20 +
    21 +Assertion:
    22 +gamma_S is conjugate by exp_p to a linear involution on T_p S ~= R^d, with eigenspace dimensions k and d-k.
    23 +
    24 +Local Context `Gamma_i`:
    25 +- gamma_1: gamma_S is an isometric involution of the Hadamard manifold S.
    26 +
    27 +Global Knowledge `Sigma_i`:
    28 +- sigma_1:
    29 +  exact as-used statement: If S is a Hadamard manifold and p is a point of S, then exp_p : T_pS -> S is a diffeo
        morphism.
    30 +  proof or precise source: Cartan-Hadamard theorem. Precise source: Manfredo do Carmo, Riemannian Geometry, Chap
        ter 6, Theorem 6.6.
    31 +  why it applies here: After constructing a fixed point p directly, this identifies S globally with T_pS and mak
        es the isometry linearizable on all of S.
    32 +  status: confirmed
    33 +
    34 +Sigma Support:
    35 +- For sigma_1: the fixed-point existence is proved directly in this step by the midpoint argument, and sigma_1 s
        upplies the global diffeomorphism exp_p needed for the linear model.
    36 +
    37 +Background Theory `T_i`:
    38 +- t_1: In a Hadamard manifold, any two points are joined by a unique geodesic segment.
    39 +- t_2: If an isometry g fixes p, then exp_p o d g_p = g o exp_p.
    40 +- t_3: An involutive linear map on a real finite-dimensional vector space has only eigenvalues +1 and -1.
    41 +
    42 +Deduction:
    43 +Choose x in S. By t_1 there is a unique geodesic c:[0,1]->S from x to gamma_S(x). Because gamma_S is an involuti
        ve isometry, gamma_S o c is the unique geodesic from gamma_S(x) to x, so gamma_S(c(t))=c(1-t). Hence p=c(1/2) is
         fixed by gamma_S. By t_2,
    44 +exp_p o d(gamma_S)_p = gamma_S o exp_p.
    45 +By confirmed sigma_1, exp_p is a diffeomorphism T_pS -> S. Therefore gamma_S is conjugate to the linear involuti

     9 +
    10 +Target assertion a_i:
    11 +The projection q : (X x S)/Gamma -> M is a fiber bundle with fiber S; in particular its fiber is contractible.
    12 +
    13 +Original route:
    14 +Use the covering projection X -> M and the diagonal Gamma-action on X x S to build local trivializations.
    15 +
    16 +Explanation:
    17 +Write pi : X -> M for the universal covering map and q([x,s]) = pi(x). Since Gamma acts freely on X by deck tran
        sformations, pi is an evenly covered covering map. Over any evenly covered open set U subset M, choose one sheet
         U~ subset X on which pi restricts to a homeomorphism U~ -> U. Every q-fiber over U then has a unique representa
        tive with first coordinate in U~, so q^{-1}(U) is canonically homeomorphic to U x S. This gives local trivializa
        tions with fiber S. The contractibility of the fiber is immediate from the contractibility of S already assumed
        in the proof.
    18 +

    26 +- gamma_2: Gamma acts continuously on S.

    36 +- t_1: A covering projection is locally trivial over evenly covered open sets.
    37 +- t_2: If U~ subset X maps homeomorphically to U subset M under pi, then every Gamma-orbit in pi^{-1}(U) meets U
        ~ in exactly one point.
    38 +
    39 +Deduction:
    40 +Fix an evenly covered open set U subset M and let U~ subset X be the chosen sheet from t_1. Define
    41 +Phi_U : U x S -> q^{-1}(U),  Phi_U(u,s) = [ (pi|_{U~})^{-1}(u), s ].
    42 +This map is continuous. To construct its inverse, take [x,s] in q^{-1}(U). By t_2 there is a unique g in Gamma w
        ith g^{-1}x in U~. Put
    43 +Psi_U([x,s]) = ( pi(x), g^{-1}s ).
    44 +If [x,s] = [hx,hs] for h in Gamma, then the unique element sending hx into U~ is hg^{-1}, so Psi_U is well defin
        ed. One checks directly that Psi_U and Phi_U are inverse homeomorphisms. Therefore q is locally trivial with fib
        er S. By gamma_3 the fiber is contractible.

     1 +Step ID:

     3 +
     4 +Why this is one minimal logical deduction unit:
     5 +This segment has one nontrivial conclusion: dim(M_0) = d = dim(S). The top-degree cohomology calculation and the
         vanishing in degrees above d are the route to that dimension equality.
     6 +
     7 +Original step:
     8 +Because $S_{0}$ is a compact orientable $d$-manifold, its top non-vanishing rational cohomology is strictly $H^{

        }$. As $f_{0}^{*}$ is an isomorphism globally in all degrees, the non-vanishing cohomologies of $M_{0}$ and $S_{
        0}$ must perfectly align, strictly forcing the global dimension of $M_{0}$ to be exactly $d$.
     9 +
    10 +Target assertion a_i:
    11 +dim(M_0) = d.

        anifolds.
    15 +
    16 +Explanation:
    17 +If S13B were available, the argument is straightforward: S_0 has nonzero rational cohomology in degree d and no
        cohomology above degree d, so the same must hold for M_0. A connected compact orientable m-manifold has nonzero
        H^m(-;Q), so m cannot exceed d; the nonzero H^d(M_0;Q) shows m cannot be smaller than d. However, this unit depe
        nds directly on the open cohomology-isomorphism step S13B.

        0;Q)=0 for j>d. If m<d, this contradicts H^d(M_0;Q) ~= Q. Hence m=d.

  Examining homotopy lifting in fibrations

  fibration property of fiber bundles over paracompact bases.

  Exploring proof techniques

  to prove my point. It’s a bit tricky with general topological bundles. However, over a closed disk, I can choose a

  local trivialization over a contractible base may not be globally trivial unless it’s paracompact. Interestingly, a
  numerable bundle over a contractible paracompact base is trivial, which is an external concept.
